1	# CODE_MAP.md
2	
3	**Purpose:** cold-start navigation map for the badminton-highlight-indexer — a self-hosted video → highlight-reel pipeline (CV/AI rally detection → SQLite → ffmpeg stitch) with a segmenter-agnostic eval/calibration stack, typed config, and per-video observability reporting.
4	
5	**Regenerate:** rerun the `build-code-map` workflow (parallel subsystem readers → this doc). Re-run after any registry/contract/WSL-interface change, or after a directory restructure (this revision follows #40).
6	
7	⚠ **2026-06-10 audit-hardening sweep changed several behaviors documented below — trust git history over stale lines until the next full regen:** `add_video` is now an **UPSERT** (no FK-cascade wipe on re-ingest; segmentation is destroy-AFTER-confirm via `segment_watermarks`/`prune_segments`); detector artifacts are keyed by **video_id**, not basename; **`indexing.wasb.*` is now a real typed config** (was silently dropped); `timeout_sec` defaults **1800s** (was 0/hang-forever); SQLite opens with **WAL + busy_timeout**; `match_intervals` requires real overlap (iou>0); `run_regression` baseline lives in tracked `eval_baselines/` and **exits 3 on a missing baseline**; the API validates `video_path`/`output_filename`. New tests: `test_api_endpoints`, `test_compiler`, `test_wasb_runner`, `test_reingest_safety`, `test_detector_keying`, `test_config_wasb`, `test_path_safety`, `test_reliability_hardening` (+ collector `test_licensing_gate`, `test_import_guard`, `test_cvat_robustness`).
8	
9	**LEGEND**
10	- `@path` = file (always repo-relative)
11	- `::sym` = symbol (class/func/const) in the nearest preceding `@path`
12	- `->` = dataflow / call / "produces"
13	- `§` = data contract (canonical shape)
14	- `⚠` = gotcha / footgun
15	- `⎇` = registry / plugin extension point
16	- `WSL` = runs inside WSL2 conda env, NOT the Windows Python process
17	
18	---
19	
20	## 1. PIPELINE
21	
22	### 1A. Runtime: video file -> highlight reel
23	```
24	typed config load (built-in defaults -> config.json overrides; absent/parse-fail -> all-defaults, never fatal)

28	       FormatValidator -> ctx['ffprobe_meta'] -> ResolutionFPSValidator -> PTSContinuity -> SceneCut -> Blur -> Relevance   (⚠ ACTUAL registration/exec order — see @backend/pipeline/validators/__init__.py)
29	       [validation FAIL -> persist status="failed", return early; report STILL emitted in finally]
30	  -> db.add_video(video_id, filepath, status, [r.dict()])   @backend/infrastructure/database.py
31	  -> seg_name = req.segmenter or global_config.indexing.default_segmenter (fallback 'motion')
32	  -> seg = segmenter_registry.get(seg_name)(db, global_config)   ⎇ @backend/pipeline/segmenters/base.py::segmenter_registry  (400 + available list if missing)
33	  -> segments = seg.process_video(video_id, video_path, player_pool?, max_frames?)
34	       [STOP POINT: segments-only — predictions now in DB; eval/regression operate here without stitching]
35	  -> finally: emit_indexer_report(...)    @backend/reporting/__init__.py  (⚠ ALWAYS runs — even on validation failure / exception; NEVER raises; grafts response["reports"])
36	  -> select segment ids -> [(start,end)] (+ stitching padding)
37	  -> VideoCompiler.compile_highlights(filepath, time_pairs, ...

1	#!/usr/bin/env python
2	"""Run the FULL test suite for the badminton-highlight-indexer.
3	
4	A single, discoverable entry point so you (or a reviewer / CI) can verify the
5	whole suite before opening a PR:
6	
7	    python run_all_tests.py             # full suite, quiet
8	    python run_all_tests.py -v          # verbose (passthrough to pytest)
9	    python run_all_tests.py -k config   # filter by keyword (passthrough)
10	    python run_all_tests.py --cov=backend --cov-report=term-missing   # coverage
11	
12	Anything after the script name is passed straight through to pytest.
13	
14	Notes
15	-----
16	- The suite is fully mocked (no GPU, no network, no WSL) and runs offline.
17	- The observability **reporting** tests skip cleanly if the optional ``obsreport``
18	  package isn't installed (they use ``pytest.importorskip``). To exercise them too:
19	      pip install -e ../sports-obsreport --no-build-isolation
20	- Exit code is pytest's own, so this is CI / pre-PR friendly (0 = all green).
21	"""
22	from __future__ import annotations
23	
24	import importlib.util
25	import subprocess
26	import sys
27	
28	
29	def _installed(module: str) -> bool:
30	    try:
31	        return importlib.util.find_spec(module) is not None
32	    except (ImportError, ValueError):
33	        return False
34	
35	
36	def main() -> int:
37	    args = list(sys.argv[1:])
38	    # Default to quiet unless the caller chose a verbosity.
39	    if not any(a in ("-q", "-v", "-vv", "-vvv", "--quiet", "--verbose") for a in args):
40	        args = ["-q", *args]
41	    cmd = [sys.executable, "-m", "pytest", *args]
42	
43	    obs = "available" if _installed("obsreport") else "NOT installed → reporting tests will SKIP"
44	    bar = "=" * 72
45	    print(bar)
46	    print(" Full test suite:  " + " ".join(cmd))
47	    print(f" obsreport (optional reporting dep): {obs}")
48	    print(bar, flush=True)
49	
50	    rc = subprocess.run(cmd).returncode
51	
52	    print(bar)
53	    print(" RESULT: " + ("PASS - all tests green" if rc == 0 else f"FAIL - pytest exit {rc}"))
54	    print(bar)
55	    return rc
56	
57	
58	if __name__ == "__main__":
59	    raise SystemExit(main())
60

### 27. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.383Z)

[external_agent_tool_result]
1	"""Pure temporal interval-matching metrics for rally evaluation.
2	
3	No I/O, no DB, no global state — every function here is deterministic and unit
4	tested. An "interval" is a `(start, end)` tuple of floats in seconds with
5	start < end. We score a list of *predicted* rally intervals against a list of
6	*ground-truth* intervals via temporal IoU and one-to-one greedy matching.
7	"""
8	
9	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
10	from typing import List, Tuple, Optional
11	
12	Interval = Tuple[float, float]
13	
14	
15	def interval_iou(a: Interval, b: Interval) -> float:
16	    """Temporal intersection-over-union of two intervals.
17	
18	    Returns 0.0 when they don't overlap (or either is degenerate). IoU is
19	    overlap / union, both measured as durations on the timeline.
20	    """
21	    a_start, a_end = a
22	    b_start, b_end = b
23	    if a_end <= a_start or b_end <= b_start:
24	        return 0.0
25	    inter = max(0.0, min(a_end, b_end) - max(a_start, b_start))
26	    if inter <= 0.0:
27	        return 0.0
28	    union = (a_end - a_start) + (b_end - b_start) - inter
29	    return inter / union if union > 0 else 0.0
30	
31	
32	@dataclass
33	class Match:
34	    """One matched predicted↔ground-truth pair."""
35	    pred_index: int
36	    gt_index: int
37	    iou: float
38	
39	
40	@dataclass
41	class MatchResult:
42	    """Outcome of matching predictions to ground truth at a given IoU threshold."""
43	    matches: List[Match] = field(default_factory=list)
44	    unmatched_pred_indices: List[int] = field(default_factory=list)  # false positives
45	    unmatched_gt_indices: List[int] = field(default_factory=list)    # false negatives (misses)
46	
47	
48	def match_intervals(preds: List[Interval], gts: List[Interval],
49	                    iou_threshold: float = 0.5) -> MatchResult:
50	    """Greedy one-to-one matching of predictions to ground truth by descending IoU.
51	
52	    Every candidate (pred, gt) pair with IoU >= threshold is considered; pairs
53	    are claimed highest-IoU first, and each prediction/GT can be used once. This
54	    keeps a single prediction from being credited for two ground-truth rallies
55	    (and vice versa).
56	    """
57	    candidates = []
58	    for pi, p in enumerate(preds):
59	        for gi, g in enumerate(gts):
60	            iou = interval_iou(p, g)
61	            # Require real overlap: at iou_threshold=0.0 a plain `iou >= threshold` would
62	            # match completely disjoint intervals (IoU 0.0), fabricating perfect scores.
63	            if iou > 0.0 and iou >= iou_threshold:
64	                candidates.append((iou, pi, gi))
65	    # Highest IoU first; ties broken by indices for determinism.
66	    candidates.sort(key=lambda c: (-c[0], c[1], c[2]))
67	
68	    used_pred, used_gt = set(), set()
69	    matches: List[Match] = []
70	    for iou, pi, gi in candidates:
71	        if pi in used_pred or gi in used_gt:
72	            continue
73	        used_pred.add(pi)
74	        used_gt.add(gi)
75	        matches.append(Match(pred_index=pi, gt_index=gi, iou=iou))
76	
77	    unmatched_pred = [i for i in range(len(preds)) if i not in used_pred]
78	    unmatched_gt = [i for i in range(len(gts)) if i not in used_gt]
79	    return MatchResult(matches=matches,
80	                       unmatched_pred_indices=unmatched_pred,
81	                       unmatched_gt_indices=unmatched_gt)
82	
83	
84	@dataclass
85	class Score:
86	    """Aggregate metrics for one (predictions vs ground-truth) evaluation."""
87	    iou_threshold: float
88	    num_pred: int
89	    num_gt: int
90	    true_positives: int
91	    false_positives: int
92	    false_negatives: int
93	    precision: float
94	    recall: float
95	    f1: float
96	    mean_iou: float            # over matched pairs (0.0 if none)
97	    mean_start_error: float    # mean |pred_start - gt_start| over matches, seconds
98	    mean_end_error: float      # mean |pred_end - gt_end| over matches, seconds
99	
100	    def as_dict(self) -> dic...

1	import sqlite3
2	import json
3	import os
4	import logging
5	from typing import List, Dict, Any, Optional, Tuple
6	
7	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
8	
9	class Database:
10	    def __init__(self, db_path: str):
11	        self.db_path = db_path
12	        self._init_db()
13	
14	    def _get_connection(self):
15	        # busy_timeout: wait (not fail) on a locked DB — needed once the async job model
16	        # adds concurrent writers (a swallowed 'database is locked' silently drops segments).
17	        conn = sqlite3.connect(self.db_path, timeout=30.0)
18	        conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
19	        # Enforce ON DELETE CASCADE / SET NULL — SQLite leaves FKs off per-connection.
20	        conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON")
21	        # WAL allows concurrent readers during a write; busy_timeout bounds lock waits.
22	        conn.execute("PRAGMA busy_timeout = 30000")
23	        try:
24	            conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL")
25	        except sqlite3.OperationalError:
26	            pass  # e.g. a read-only/networked FS that can't do WAL — degrade gracefully
27	        return conn
28	
29	    def _init_db(self):
30	        """Initializes tables for videos, play_segments, and events."""
31	        with self._get_connection() as conn:
32	            cursor = conn.cursor()
33	            
34	            # Videos Table
35	            cursor.execute("""
36	                CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS videos (
37	                    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
38	                    filepath TEXT NOT NULL,
39	                    processed_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
40	                    status TEXT NOT NULL,
41	                    validation_results TEXT
42	                )
43	            """)
44	
45	            # Play Segments Table (Extensible metadata JSON)
46	            cursor.execute("""
47	                CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS play_segments (
48	                    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
49	                    video_id TEXT NOT NULL,
50	                    start_time REAL NOT NULL,
51	                    end_time REAL NOT NULL,
52	                    duration REAL NOT NULL,
53	                    server TEXT,
54	                    receiver TEXT,
55	                    metadata TEXT,
56	                    FOREIGN KEY (video_id) REFERENCES videos(id) ON DELETE CASCADE
57	                )
58	            """)
59	
60	            # Events Table
61	            cursor.execute("""
62	                CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS events (
63	                    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
64	                    segment_id INTEGER NOT NULL,
65	                    timestamp REAL NOT NULL,
66	                    type TEXT NOT NULL,
67	                    player TEXT,
68	                    details TEXT,
69	                    FOREIGN KEY (segment_id) REFERENCES play_segments(id) ON DELETE CASCADE
70	                )
71	            """)
72	
73	            # Versioned migrations live here. PRAGMA user_version is the schema
74	            # contract — bump it for every change and add an ordered `if version < N`
75	            # block below. Tests assert the expected version, so accidental drift fails CI.
76	            version = cursor.execute("PRAGMA user_version").fetchone()[0]
77	
78	            if version < 1:
79	                # v1: raw candidate detections (pre-confirmation), detector-agnostic.
80	                # Common fields are columns; detector-specific metrics go in `stats` JSON,
81	                # so a new segmenter reuses this table by setting its own `detector`/`stats`.
82	                cursor.execute("""
83	                    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS candidate_segments (
84	                        id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
85	                        video_id TEXT NOT NULL,
86	                        detector TEXT NOT NULL,
87	                        chunk_index INTEGER,
88	                        start_time REAL NOT NULL,

Init done — I read the handoff + current-state, ramped on [CODE_MAP.md](docs/CODE_MAP.md), and then gathered fresh evidence rather than relying on memory: ran the full suite (**349 passed, 0 failed, 40s**, fully offline), read the CI workflow, sampled core modules ([metrics.py](backend/eval/metrics.py), [database.py](backend/infrastructure/database.py)), and checked tooling configs and git history (189 commits since 2026-05-18).

## Overall: 8/10 — unusually disciplined for a pre-product solo repo

The headline: this repo's **process and knowledge-management rigor is well above industry norm**, its architecture is genuinely good, and its weaknesses are mostly *known, documented, and deliberately deferred* rather than unknown — which is itself a quality signal. The one undeniable hole is static analysis: there is no linter, formatter, or type-checker anywhere.

| Dimension | Score | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Architecture & design | 9 | Five pluggable registry seams (segmenters/providers/validators/sports/annotations), ABC contracts, typed Pydantic config with single-sourced defaults, `PRAGMA user_version` migration ladder, train==serve featurizer behind a CI-enforced import wall |
| Documentation | 9.5 | CODE_MAP with data contracts + footgun markers, 9 ADRs, archives/checkpoints, honest negative results recorded (audio E1 rejected, failed heuristics kept) |
| Process & governance | 9 | One-PR-per-item, conventional commits, merges gated on verified-green CI, licensing gate that *actually caught* violations (ShuttleSet reclassification), audit→12-PR remediation loop |
| Eval/ML rigor | 9 | Honest LOVO, leakage guards, midrank-tie AUC, committed baselines in `eval_baselines/`, regression gate with semantic exit codes, results independently reproduced |
| Testing | 8 | 349 green in 40s, 51 modules, ~3.9k test lines vs ~8.2k source, negative-path tests (re-ingest safety, path traversal, detector keying) |
| Code quality | 7.5 | Sampled modules are clean with *why*-comments; minus for documented copy-paste twins and legacy shims |
| CI/CD | 6.5 | Smart two-lane design (import-smoke without GPU stack + full suite), least-privilege, concurrency-cancel — but only 1 day old and no lint/type/coverage lane |
| Security | 7 | API path validation, gitignored secrets, `permissions: contents: read`; pending chat-pasted Gemini key rotation (tracked) |
| Static analysis tooling | 3 | No ruff/flake8/black/mypy/pre-commit, nothing in CI |
| Packaging | 4 | No `pyproject.toml`, not pip-installable — deferred with intent in BACKLOG, and it already cost you (it drove the repo-split analysis) |

## What's genuinely excellent

The **honesty loop** is the standout. Most repos this age have aspirational docs; this one has a CODE_MAP whose header says "trust git history over stale lines," a motion segmenter explicitly labeled as fabricating random metadata, and an audit trail where the project found its own master broken (the compiler SyntaxError that 254 green tests never noticed), fixed it, added CI specifically shaped to catch that class again (`test_import_smoke.py` does a `py_compile` sweep plus a stubbed `import backend.main`), and wrote the lesson down. The eval culture — committed baselines, LOVO instead of fit-on-self numbers, a ship-gate that honestly reported `ship=False` on its own first model — is rarer than good code.

The sampled code backs the docs up: [metrics.py](backend/eval/metrics.py:63) has a deliberate `iou > 0.0` guard with a comment explaining the fabricated-perfect-score failure mode it prevents, deterministic tie-breaking, and pure no-I/O design; [database.py](backend/infrastructure/database.py:73) has a versioned migration ladder with test-asserted schema version and graceful WAL degradation.

## Where it loses points

1. **No static analysis at all** — the biggest gap, and the cheapest to close. The suite output itself shows the cost: 15 Pydantic deprecation warnings (`.dict()` → `model_dump()` at [main.py:168](backend/main.py:168), 157, 162, 221) that a ruff lane would have flagged. A `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` job in CI is an afternoon of work.
2. **Documented-but-live duplication** — `tracknet_hybrid`/`wasb_hybrid` are copy-paste twins where "any P3/P4 fix must be applied to BOTH," `_wsl_bash` is duplicated across runners, and padding constants are duplicated between the compiler and rally_slicer. The CODE_MAP flags all of these, but flags don't prevent drift.
3. **Legacy tolerance footguns** — `extra='allow'` on AppConfig keeps typo'd config keys silently, the dict-compat `.get()` shim and the dict-returning legacy `load_config` are still load-bearing, and two incompatible GT CSV conventions coexist (mitigated by the canonical `gt_loader`, but the old loaders still exist).
4. **Small hygiene nits**: [.coveragerc](.coveragerc) omits paths that no longer exist (`backend/indexer/motion.py`, `backend/validators/*` — pre-restructure layout), and `TEST_RUN_SUMMARY.md` is a tracked point-in-time artifact at the root.
5. **CI is young** — the repo ran its first ~3 weeks without CI, and the coverage-inversion lesson (tests dense where risk was low) was only corrected in the 2026-06-10 sweep. Current state is good; the track record is short.

## If you want to convert 8 → 9

In priority order: (1) a ruff + mypy CI lane with the two-line `.dict()` fixes, (2) `pyproject.toml` packaging, (3) collapse the hybrid-segmenter twins into one parameterized base, (4) flip `extra='allow'` to `'forbid'` once the legacy shim retires. All four are already implicitly tracked in your own BACKLOG/deferred-hardening docs — which is rather the point: this repo knows where its bodies are buried, and that's what makes it an 8 rather than a 6.5 with the same code.

Happy to turn any of these into a PR-sized work item if you want — say the word (per the workflow agreement, I won't start one unprompted).

## Proposed action items (8 → 9)

**Quick wins**

1. **AI-1 · Repo hygiene** (S, no risk) — Fix the stale [.coveragerc](.coveragerc) omit paths (`backend/indexer/`, `backend/validators/` no longer exist post-restructure; repoint to their current `backend/pipeline/` equivalents). Untrack `TEST_RUN_SUMMARY.md` (point-in-time artifact) and gitignore it — after checking nothing references it. Also sweep the stale pre-restructure `__pycache__` dirs locally (not a PR concern, just dirt).
2. **AI-2 · Pydantic v2 cleanup** (S, low risk) — Replace the four deprecated `.dict()` calls in [main.py](backend/main.py:157) with `model_dump()`, plus a grep for any other Pydantic v1 idioms (`.json()`, `parse_obj`). Kills the 15 deprecation warnings in the suite; removes a v3 time bomb.

**CI quality lanes** (each becomes a new job in [ci.yml](.github/workflows/ci.yml))

3. **AI-3 · Ruff lint lane** (M, low-med risk) — Add `ruff.toml` (target py38 to match the stated floor; pragmatic ruleset: pyflakes/pycodestyle errors + import sorting + bugbear + pyupgrade, with per-file-ignores where the code is deliberate, e.g. CLI `print`s). Fix all violations (mostly auto-fixable; diff is wide but mechanical), add a `lint` CI job. **No `ruff format` enforcement** — a whole-repo reformat would be a noise bomb; lint only.
4. **AI-4 · Mypy lane** (M, medium risk on scope) — Lenient baseline config (`ignore_missing_imports`, exclude the WSL-only `wasb_infer.py` if needed), fix the *real* errors it surfaces, add CI job. Scoped to land green now and ratchet stricter later — not a typing crusade.
5. **AI-5 · Coverage report in CI** (S, low risk) — Run the full-suite job with `--cov`, print the term-missing summary, and set a conservative `fail-under` floor just below current measured coverage so it can only ratchet up. Directly addresses the historical coverage-inversion blind spot.

**Structural**

6. **AI-6 · Config unknown-key warning** (S-M, low risk) — The loader currently keeps typo'd top-level keys silently (`extra='allow'`) and nested sections silently *drop* unknown keys. Add a loud `[CONFIG WARNING] unknown keys: …` at load time for both, preserving the never-fatal contract. The full `extra='forbid'` flip stays deferred until the deprecated `gemini_*` aliases retire (that's a behavior decision, not a cleanup).
7. **AI-7 · Collapse the hybrid-segmenter twins** (M-L, medium risk — the big one) — Extract a shared trajectory-hybrid base; `tracknet_hybrid`/`wasb_hybrid` become thin subclasses (runner + config section + params). Resolves the documented `shot_count` copy-paste drift deliberately (align both to the safe is-None short-circuit), dedupes `_wsl_bash` into the detectors layer, and adds equivalence tests so the twins can never drift again. Goes last so ruff/mypy/coverage lanes guard it.
8. **AI-8 · `pyproject.toml` packaging** (M, medium risk) — PEP 621 metadata, setuptools backend, dependencies sourced dynamically from `requirements.txt` (single source). Package `backend` **only** — excluding `training/` from the wheel reinforces ADR-008's import wall. One wrinkle: the existing [setup.py](setup.py) is a bootstrap script, not setuptools — it must be renamed (e.g. `scripts/setup_env.py`) or `pip install` would execute it with side effects; I'll update doc references. Package name stays `badminton-highlight-indexer` for now — trivially renamed when the repo rename lands, no point prejudging it.

**Execution protocol:** sequential, one PR at a time off fresh master; suite green locally before push; merge gated on `statusCheckRollup == SUCCESS` (the #75/#76 lesson); squash-merge + branch delete; re-check PR state before any follow-up push (the #80 lesson). Your two uncommitted PR-2 doc edits on master stay untouched throughout — selective `git add` only, never `-A`.

1	# Test Run Diagnosis & Optimizations Summary
2	
3	> **Historical note:** This is a point-in-time diagnosis from a May 2026 local test run,
4	> written *before* the layered-directory restructure (PR #40). Path references below

7	> lessons remain valid; treat the file paths and install commands as historical.

9	*Date: May 18, 2026*  
10	*Target File: `C:\Users\avidu\Videos\LargeTestVideo.MP4` (HEVC H.265, Size: ~11.5 GB)*
11	
12	This summary details the findings, architectural lessons, and performance optimizations implemented during today's local test run of the Badminton Highlight & Rally Indexer.
13	
14	---
15	
16	## 🔍 Why the Initial Test Run Blocked (and How We Resolved It)
17	
18	During the raw test execution on the 11.5 GB video stream, the system hit three distinct, high-impact constraints. We fully diagnosed, optimized, and hardened the pipelines to address them:
19	
20	### 1. The Keyframe Extraction Gap (H.265 Stream Header Parsing)
21	*   **The Issue**: The `PTSContinuityValidator` check initially failed with `[FAIL] No keyframes could be extracted`.
22	*   **The Root Cause**: GoPro raw H.265/HEVC encodes use **Gradual Decoder Refresh (GDR)** or **Clean Random Access (CRA)** keyframe configurations. Standard FFmpeg frame-level decoding with `-skip_frame nokey` relies on hardware/software decoders which failed to flag standard keyframe structures, returning empty outputs.
23	*   **The Optimization**: We changed the validator to query packet indexes (`packet=pts_time,flags`) at the **container demuxer level** instead of full frame decoding. Because packet flags (e.g. `K__`) are written directly to container headers, this bypasses the video decoder entirely. It runs **100x faster** and is 100% robust across HEVC containers!
24	
25	### 2. The Blurriness Quality Barrier (Indoor Arena Low-Light Noise)
26	*   **The Issue**: The video failed the focus threshold check with a sharpness score of `35.3` (minimum threshold was `100.0`).
27	*   **The Root Cause**: High-speed action footage in typical indoor badminton environments suffers from high-speed motion blur and ISO sensor noise, reducing the computed Laplacian variance.
28	*   **The Fix**: We lowered the `min_blur_threshold` default inside `config.json` to `20.0` so that raw fast-moving arena footage passes quality checks cleanly.
29	
30	### 3. OpenCV Seeking Performance Bottleneck

10	- `past_projects/` — Completed small tech debt, starter projects, or low-regret initiatives (e.g. the original low-regret moves + scaffolding work). These are "past" once they reach a terminal state.

152	Notable knobs:
153	*   `validation.min_blur_threshold` — defaults to `20.0` (deliberately permissive for noisy indoor-arena GoPro footage; see `TEST_RUN_SUMMARY.md` for why).
154	*   `indexing.default_segmenter` — `yolo_hybrid` | `gemini` | `motion`.
155	*   `indexing.use_gpu` — when true and CUDA is available, YOLO runs on GPU.
156	*   `indexing.yolo_velocity_threshold` — fraction of frame width per second; lower values catch slower play, higher values are stricter about what counts as "action".
157	*   `indexing.yolo_frame_skip` — process every Nth frame during YOLO tracking. `3` at 30 fps ≈ 10 samples/sec.
158	*   `indexing.chunk_duration` / `chunk_overlap` — how to split long videos for the YOLO phase.
159	*   `indexing.ai_handoff_padding` — seconds of slack added around each YOLO candidate window before sending to the AI provider.

**AI-2 of the approved 8->9 quality sweep.**

## What
Replaces the 7 deprecated `.dict()` calls in `backend/main.py` (all `[r.dict() for r in val_results]` on `ValidationResult`) with `model_dump()`, the direct v2 successor. `.dict()` is removed in pydantic v3, so this was a time bomb on any future dependency bump.

Scope was grepped repo-wide for the other v1 idioms (`.json()` on models, `parse_obj`, `parse_raw`, v1 `@validator`, `construct`) - none exist; the `.json()` hits in tests are httpx `Response.json()`, not pydantic.

115	    """Leave-one-video-out CV — the honest metric as the corpus grows.
116	
117	    `videos`: list of {"name": str, "predict_fn": PredictFn, "gts": [Interval]}.
118	    For each held-out video: pick the config that's best *pooled across the other
119	    videos*, then score it on the held-out one. The mean held-out score is the
120	    "precision on unseen videos" number; per-video spread shows stability.
121	    """
122	    if len(videos) < 2:
123	        return {"error": "need >=2 videos for leave-one-video-out", "n": len(videos)}
124	    per_video = []
125	    for i, held in enumerate(videos):
126	        others = [v for j, v in enumerate(videos) if j != i]
127	
128	        def pooled_score(cfg: Config) -> float:
129	            vals = [getattr(score(v["predict_fn"](cfg), v["gts"], iou_threshold), metric)
130	                    for v in others]
131	            return _st.mean(vals)
132	
133	        best_cfg, best_train = max(((c, pooled_score(c)) for c in configs), key=lambda t: t[1])
134	        held_score = getattr(score(held["predict_fn"](best_cfg), held["gts"], iou_threshold), metric)
135	        per_video.append({"video": held.get("name", f"video_{i}"),
136	                          "held_out": held_score, "train_pooled": best_train, "config": best_cfg})
137	    held_scores = [p["held_out"] for p in per_video]
138	    train_scores = [p["train_pooled"] for p in per_video]
139	    return {
140	        "metric": metric,
141	        "mean_held_out": _st.mean(held_scores),
142	        "std_held_out": _st.pstdev(held_scores) if len(held_scores) > 1 else 0.0,
143	        "generalization_gap": _st.mean(train_scores) - _st.mean(held_scores),
144	        "per_video": per_video,
[/external_agent_tool_result]

50	    Speed is normalized to fraction-of-frame-width PER SECOND (``* fps``), matching the
51	    heuristic windowing brain — previously it was per-frame, so the same physical shuttle
52	    speed read ~2x smaller at 59.94 fps than at 30 fps, confounding cross-footage transfer
53	    on a corpus that mixes frame rates. Speed/crossings are also not computed across a long
54	    occlusion gap (mirrors the heuristic resetting on invisibility)."""
55	    pts = sorted(points, key=lambda p: p.frame)
56	    N = (pts[-1].frame + 1) if pts else 1
57	    vis = np.zeros(N); x = np.zeros(N); y = np.zeros(N)
58	    for p in pts:
59	        if 0 <= p.frame < N and p.visible and p.x >= 0:
60	            vis[p.frame] = 1.0; x[p.frame] = float(p.x); y[p.frame] = float(p.y)
61	
62	    mask = vis > 0
63	    play_is_x = _spread(x[mask]) >= _spread(y[mask])
64	    coord = x if play_is_x else y
65	    net = float(np.median(coord[mask])) if mask.any() else 0.0
66	    deadband = 0.06 * (_spread(coord[mask]) if mask.sum() > 1 else 1.0)
67	
68	    speed = np.zeros(N); spd_mask = np.zeros(N); cross = np.zeros(N)
69	    max_gap = max(2, round(0.25 * fps))  # frames; a longer gap = occlusion break, not motion
70	    prev = None
71	    for p in pts:
72	        f = p.frame
73	        if not (0 <= f < N and vis[f]):
74	            continue
75	        if prev is not None:
76	            df = f - prev
77	            if 0 < df <= max_gap:
78	                speed[f] = float(np.hypot(x[f] - x[prev], y[f] - y[prev]) / frame_width / df * fps)
79	                spd_mask[f] = 1.0
80	                c, pc = coord[f] - net, coord[prev] - net
81	                if abs(c) > deadband and abs(pc) > deadband and (c > 0) != (pc > 0):
82	                    cross[f] = 1.0
83	        prev = f
84	    return dict(N=N, vis=vis, x=x, y=y, speed=speed, spd_mask=spd_mask, cross=cross, fw=frame_width)
85

64	
65	
66	def sign_test(learned: List[float], floor: List[float]):
67	    """Paired one-sided sign test: is learned > floor more often than chance?
68	
69	    At n=6 a mean comparison alone is inside noise (SE ~0.11); the paired per-video
70	    wins are the defensible statistic. Returns (wins, n, p_one_sided)."""
71	    pairs = [(l, f) for l, f in zip(learned, floor) if l != f]  # ties drop, standard
72	    wins = sum(1 for l, f in pairs if l > f)
73	    n = len(pairs)
74	    p = sum(comb(n, k) for k in range(wins, n + 1)) / (2 ** n) if n else 1.0
75	    return wins, n, p
76	
77	
78	def train_final(specs: List[Dict]):
79	    """Fit the FINAL model on ALL corpus videos; threshold tuned on the same (train-side).
80	
81	    The honest generalization estimate is the LOVO result, not this fit — recorded as such."""
82	    vids = []
83	    for s in specs:
84	        pts = TrackNetRunner.parse_trajectory_csv(s["trajectory"])
85	        arr = build_frame_arrays(pts, float(s["frame_width"]), float(s["fps"]))
86	        X, times = features(arr, float(s["fps"]))
87	        gts = load_gt_intervals(s["labels"], strict=False)
88	        vids.append({"name": s["name"], "X": X, "times": times,
89	                     "y": labels_for(times, gts), "gts": gts})
90	    Xall = np.vstack([v["X"] for v in vids])
91	    yall = np.concatenate([v["y"] for v in vids])
92	    clf = LogisticRegression(lr=0.2, epochs=2000, l2=1e-2)
93	    clf.fit(Xall, yall, feature_names=FEAT_NAMES)
94	    thr = max(THRESHOLD_GRID, key=lambda t: _seg_f1(clf, vids, float(t)))
95	    return clf, float(thr)
96	
97	
98	def gate_verdict(eval_res: dict, floor: Optional[dict]) -> dict:
99	    """Pre-flight ship-gate. ship=True requires EVERY block to clear — and two blocks
100	    (undefined F1@tIoU target; WASB C3 provenance) are open project-wide, so today this
101	    can only ever say 'floor passed, not shippable yet'. That is by design: honesty
102	    over a green light."""
103	    reasons: List[str] = []
104	    floor_passed = None
105	    sign = None
106	    if floor:
107	        floor_mean = float(floor["mean_f1"])
108	        floor_passed = eval_res["mean_f1"] > floor_mean
109	        if not floor_passed:
110	            reasons.append(f"LOVO mean {eval_res['mean_f1']:.3f} <= floor {floor_mean:.3f}")
111	        per = floor.get("per_video", {})
112	        paired = [(r["f1"], per[r["video"]]) for r in eval_res["per_video"] if r["video"] in per]
113	        if paired:
114	            wins, n, p = sign_test([l for l, _ in paired], [f for _, f in paired])
115	            sign = {"wins": wins, "n": n, "p_one_sided": round(p, 4)}
116	            if p >= 0.05:
117	                reasons.append(f"paired sign test not significant ({wins}/{n} wins, p={p:.3f}) "
118	                               f"— grow the corpus before claiming superiority")
[/external_agent_tool_result]

description: Open PR for ruff lint lane
command: gh pr create --title "ci: ruff lint lane + fix all violations; remove never-compiled flyer stub" --body @'
**AI-3 of the approved 8->9 quality sweep.**

## Headline finding
`scripts/generate_khelacharya_flyer.py` has **never compiled** since it was committed: its line 39 is a literal AI-placeholder sentence ("[Full script body would be inserted here in a real call - truncated...]"). Nothing ever swept `scripts/` (the import-smoke py_compile sweep covers `backend/` only), so it sat broken and invisible - the same class as the 7fbbeb0 compiler incident. **Removed** (the real script body never existed in git, so there is nothing to fix). The new lint lane now syntax-checks every tracked `.py` repo-wide, closing this hole permanently.

## The lane
- `ruff.toml`: ruff defaults (pyflakes + pycodestyle errors) + **W** (whitespace) + **B** (bugbear), `target-version = py38` (the stated floor). Lint-only - **no `ruff format`** (a whole-repo reformat would bury blame for zero behavior value). `scratch/` excluded (throwaway debug scripts, same as pytest.ini). E402 per-file-ignored for the three entrypoints where setup-before-import order is load-bearing.
- New `lint` CI job (runs in ~20s, parallel to the other two lanes).
- **Deferred with intent** (documented in ruff.toml): `I` (import-sort, 106 files) and `UP` (pyupgrade, ~500 hits) - large mechanical diffs, separate PR if wanted.

## Violations fixed (194 total: 152 auto, 42 manual)
- Auto: 43 dead imports, placeholder f-strings, ~180 blank-line-whitespace.
- Exception chaining (`raise ... from e`): main.py HTTPException handlers, compiler ffmpeg errors, segmenter-registry name resolution.
- **B023 in `calibration.py` LOVO**: `pooled_score` closed over the loop variable `others` - bound via default arg. Latent trap rather than live bug (the closure is consumed inside the iteration today), but this is the exact pattern that silently breaks under refactoring.
- Semicolon-joined statements split (incl. `backend/features/rally_seq.py` - the train==serve featurizer; pure line splits, suite-verified).
- Dead locals dropped: gemini.py compression-era leftovers, compiler subprocess results.
- Tests: `pytest.raises(ValidationError)` replaces blind `pytest.raises(Exception)` (B017); `assert False` -> `pytest.raises` (B011); `l` -> `lv`/`fv`/`ln` renames (E741); unused `db =` bindings dropped (F841).
- `DetectorRunner` re-export made explicit via `__all__`.

## Verification
- `ruff check .` - All checks passed.
- `python run_all_tests.py` - **349 passed** before and after every step.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

ci: add mypy lane (lenient green baseline + explicit quarantine list)

Config (mypy.ini): ignore_missing_imports + explicit_package_bases
(backend/ is a namespace package - no root __init__.py). Seven modules
quarantined with documented reasons: main.py (Optional module globals,
fixed properly by the deferred app-factory refactor), the five hybrid/
legacy segmenters (slated for the AI-7 twin collapse), and the segmenter
registry (cls(None, {}) probe needs a Protocol). Ratchet rule in the
header: try removing an entry whenever you touch a quarantined module.

Fixed the 18 errors outside the quarantine (all annotation-level, zero
behavior change):
- implicit-Optional defaults made explicit (paths, video, hit_detector,
  wasb_infer, distill_local) - also resolves the int|None -> int
  arg-type complaints in gemini_refine via extract_video_chunk
- calibrate_local: windows dict-list no longer rebound to tuple-list
  (mypy infers from first binding)
- gt_loader: assert narrows the Optional column tuple (every branch
  above assigns it); harness/validators: Dict[str, Any] annotations
- distill_local: assert final.w is not None after fit()

CI job installs typed core deps (pydantic/fastapi/numpy/...) so mypy
checks against real signatures instead of Any-stubs.

Verification: mypy green (75 files), ruff green, suite 349 green.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
'@ && git push -u origin ci/mypy-lane && gh pr create --title "ci: add mypy lane (lenient green baseline + explicit quarantine list)" --body @'
**AI-4 of the approved 8->9 quality sweep.**

## The lane
- `mypy.ini`: lenient baseline - `ignore_missing_imports` + `explicit_package_bases` (backend/ is a namespace package). **Green today** across all 75 source files.
- Honesty mechanism: instead of disabling error codes globally (which would make the gate theater), the 7 known-loose modules are **quarantined per-module with documented reasons** - main.py (Optional globals -> deferred app-factory refactor), the hybrid/legacy segmenters (-> AI-7 twin collapse), the registry probe pattern. The header states the ratchet rule: try removing an entry whenever you touch a quarantined module.
- CI job installs the typed core deps (pydantic/fastapi/numpy/...) so the check runs against real signatures, matching what local runs see.

## Real fixes outside the quarantine (18 errors, all annotation-level)
- Implicit-`Optional` parameter defaults made explicit across 5 modules; fixing `extract_video_chunk` also resolved two downstream `int | None` arg-type errors in `gemini_refine`.
- `calibrate_local.make_local_predict_fn`: stopped rebinding the action-window dict-list to a tuple-list (separate name; same values).
- `gt_loader`: assert-narrowed the Optional column tuple; `harness.report_to_dict` + validator context got `Dict[str, Any]` annotations; `distill_local` asserts `final.w` is set after `fit()`.

No behavior change anywhere - annotations, asserts on invariants that already held, and one variable rename.

## Verification
- `mypy backend training` - Success, no issues in 75 files.
- `ruff check .` - clean. `python run_all_tests.py` - **349 passed**.

10	        self.db_path = db_path

13	    def _get_connection(self):
14	        # busy_timeout: wait (not fail) on a locked DB — needed once the async job model
15	        # adds concurrent writers (a swallowed 'database is locked' silently drops segments).
16	        conn = sqlite3.connect(self.db_path, timeout=30.0)
17	        conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
18	        # Enforce ON DELETE CASCADE / SET NULL — SQLite leaves FKs off per-connection.
19	        conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON")
20	        # WAL allows concurrent readers during a write; busy_timeout bounds lock waits.
21	        conn.execute("PRAGMA busy_timeout = 30000")
22	        try:
23	            conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL")
24	        except sqlite3.OperationalError:
25	            pass  # e.g. a read-only/networked FS that can't do WAL — degrade gracefully
26	        return conn

description: Commit and open PR for DB connection fix
command: git add backend/infrastructure/database.py && git commit -m @'
fix(db): actually close SQLite connections (transaction scope != close)

`with sqlite3.connect(...)` is only a commit/rollback scope - it never
closes the connection, so every Database method leaked its connection
to GC (340 ResourceWarnings across one suite run; lingering file
handles on the WAL sidecars under Windows). New `_connect()` context
manager preserves the exact transaction semantics (`with conn:` inside)
and closes in `finally`; all 24 internal call sites switched
mechanically. `_get_connection()` kept as-is for direct test use.

Surfaced while wiring the coverage lane (AI-5) - coverage`s gc made the
warnings visible. Suite: 349 green, warnings 340 -> 4.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
'@ && git push -u origin fix/db-connection-close && gh pr create --title "fix(db): actually close SQLite connections (transaction scope != close)" --body @'
**Surfaced by AI-5 prep** (coverage instrumentation made the GC-time warnings visible); shipped as its own micro-PR so the new coverage lane starts with clean logs.

## What
`with sqlite3.connect(...)` is a commit/rollback scope only - it **never closes**. Every `Database` method therefore leaked its connection to GC: 340 `ResourceWarning: unclosed database` per suite run, and on Windows that means file handles lingering on the WAL `-wal`/`-shm` sidecars.

Fix: a `_connect()` contextmanager that wraps the same transaction scope (`with conn:`) and closes in `finally`. All 24 internal call sites switched mechanically (`_get_connection` -> `_connect`); `_get_connection()` itself is unchanged and still used directly by a few tests that manage their own scope.

## Verification
- Suite: **349 green**; ResourceWarnings under coverage: **340 -> 4** (the rest are tests using `_get_connection` directly).
- ruff + mypy clean on the touched file.
- Transaction semantics preserved exactly - commit-on-success/rollback-on-exception behavior is the same CM as before, just with a close around it.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

[external_agent_tool_result]
1	"""Configuration loader and saver.
2	
3	Provides a single place to load validated AppConfig with built-in defaults
4	merged with optional file overrides (config.json).
5	
6	This replaces scattered json.load + .get() patterns throughout the codebase.
7	"""
8	
9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	import json
12	import logging
13	from pathlib import Path
14	from typing import Any
15	
16	from .models import AppConfig
17	
18	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
19	
20	DEFAULT_CONFIG_PATH = Path("config.json")
21	
22	
23	def _get_builtin_defaults() -> dict[str, Any]:
24	    """Return a plain dict of the current built-in defaults.
25	
26	    The AppConfig model is the single source of truth for defaults.
27	    """
28	    return AppConfig().model_dump(mode="json")
29	
30	
31	def load_config(path: str | Path | None = None) -> AppConfig:
32	    """Load configuration with the following precedence:
33	
34	    1. Built-in defaults defined in the Pydantic models.
35	    2. Values from the JSON file (if present and readable).
36	    3. (Future) environment / CLI overrides.
37	
38	    Missing file or unreadable file → returns a fully defaulted AppConfig.
39	    Unknown keys in the file are tolerated (extra="allow" on the model).
40	    """
41	    cfg_path = Path(path) if path is not None else DEFAULT_CONFIG_PATH
42	
43	    file_data: dict[str, Any] = {}
44	    if cfg_path.exists():
45	        try:
46	            with open(cfg_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
47	                file_data = json.load(f)
48	        except Exception as exc:
49	            # Non-fatal: continue with defaults + whatever we could parse.
50	            # In production we might log here.
51	            logger.warning(f"Failed to read {cfg_path}: {exc}. Using defaults + partial data.")
52	
53	    # Merge: start from defaults, overlay file data (Pydantic will validate/coerce).
54	    merged = {**_get_builtin_defaults(), **file_data}
55	
56	    return AppConfig.model_validate(merged)
57	
58	
59	def save_default_config(path: str | Path | None = None) -> Path:
60	    """Write a fresh default config.json.
61	
62	    Used by setup.py diagnostics and the --setup CLI path.
63	    Overwrites if the file exists.
64	    """
65	    cfg_path = Path(path) if path is not None else DEFAULT_CONFIG_PATH
66	    cfg = AppConfig()
67	    cfg_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
68	    cfg_path.write_text(cfg.model_dump_json(indent=4), encoding="utf-8")
69	    return cfg_path
70	
71	
72	# Backwards-compatible alias used by some older call sites during transition.
73	def get_default_config() -> dict[str, Any]:

description: Commit, push, open PR 96 and watch CI
command: git add backend/config/loader.py tests/test_config.py && git commit -m @'
feat(config): warn loudly on unknown config.json keys (dotted paths)

Two silent failure modes existed: a typo'd TOP-LEVEL key is kept by
AppConfig extra='allow' but never read by typed code; a typo'd key
INSIDE a section is silently dropped by Pydantic. Either way the knob
becomes a no-op with zero feedback (the audit's silently-dropped-config
class - the indexing.wasb block was unconfigurable for weeks this way).

load_config now walks file_data against the model tree (recursing into
BaseModel-typed sections, so nested-of-nested like validation.relevance
is covered) and logs one [CONFIG WARNING] listing every unrecognized
dotted path. Never-fatal contract preserved; the repo's own config.json
is fully recognized (verified - no warning on a default run).

Full extra='forbid' stays deferred until the legacy gemini_* aliases
retire (behavior decision, tracked in BACKLOG).

Suite: 351 green (2 new tests).

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
'@ && git push -u origin feat/config-unknown-key-warning && gh pr create --title "feat(config): warn loudly on unknown config.json keys (dotted paths)" --body @'
**AI-6 of the approved 8->9 quality sweep.**

## What
`load_config` now compares the parsed `config.json` against the typed model tree and emits a single `[CONFIG WARNING]` listing every unrecognized key as a dotted path (`indexing.velocty_threshold`, `validation.relevance.unknown_gate`, ...).

## Why
Two silent failure modes: top-level typos are kept by `extra="allow"` but never read; section-level typos are silently **dropped** by Pydantic. Both make a knob a no-op with zero feedback - the exact class the audit flagged (the whole `indexing.wasb` block was unconfigurable this way before #66).

## Notes
- Never-fatal contract preserved (warning only); the repo''s own `config.json` is fully recognized - verified no warning fires on a default run.
- The full `extra="forbid"` flip stays deferred until the legacy `gemini_*` aliases retire - that''s a behavior decision, not a cleanup.

## Verification
- 2 new tests (typo'd keys at all three nesting levels warn; clean config does not). Suite **351 green**; ruff + mypy clean.

1	"""TrackNet-based hybrid play segmenter.
2	
3	Same three-phase shape as HybridYoloSegmenter, but Phase 2 (candidate
4	generation) uses a TrackNetV4 *shuttle* trajectory instead of YOLO *person*
5	motion. TrackNet runs in WSL2 (see backend/detectors/tracknet_runner.py and
6	docs/DECISIONS.md ADR-001); the shuttle trajectory is converted into candidate
7	rally windows, which are then handed to the AI provider for semantic boundary
8	confirmation (Phase 3) and written to the DB (Phase 4).
9	
10	Rationale: a tiny, fast, motion-blurred shuttle is exactly what general
11	detectors miss; a purpose-built trajectory model localises it far more
12	precisely, giving cleaner candidate rallies than person-velocity heuristics.
13	"""
14	
15	import os
16	import time
17	import logging
18	import concurrent.futures
19	from typing import List, Dict, Any
20	
21	import cv2
22	
23	from backend.pipeline.segmenters.base import BasePlaySegmenter, segmenter_registry

1	"""Abstract base for detector runners (TrackNet, WASB, future native, etc.).
2	
3	This completes the Detector Runner Abstraction (low-regret item 6 continuation / item 1
4	of the post-observability re-prioritization) to de-risk the WSL/conda dependency for
5	the high-accuracy shuttle trajectory substrate used by tracknet_hybrid / wasb_hybrid.
6	
7	The ABC lets us:
8	- Keep the current WSL adapters working unchanged for Windows users.
9	- Swap in (or A/B test) a Linux-native implementation later with zero changes to
10	  the hybrid segmenters or the model-agnostic windowing brain.
11	- Provide a single place to evolve the healthcheck / error contract and thread
12	  detector readiness into per-video reports.
13	
14	Contract (deliberately matches what the concrete runners + hybrids + tests already did):
15	- run_predict(video_win_path: str, output_win_dir: str) -> Optional[str]
16	  Run detector, return the Windows-side path to the produced trajectory CSV
17	  (e.g. ".../clip_predict.csv" or ".../clip_wasb.csv"), or None on any failure.
18	  The caller (hybrid) is responsible for output dir; impls may stage the video
19	  into WSL fs for perf.
20	
21	- healthcheck() -> tuple[bool, str]
22	  Return (ok, message). Never raises. "ok" decides whether to proceed to run_predict.
23	  Message is logged (and can be surfaced to reports or UX in future).
24	  Intended to be cheap (env existence, import, GPU visibility, weights present).
25	
26	Existing behavior is 100% preserved. The only additions are the inheritance and
27	the explicit common type for future implementers.
28	
29	How to add a new runner (for docs / future contributors):
30	1. Subclass DetectorRunner in a new or existing module under pipeline/detectors/.
31	2. Implement run_predict and healthcheck (return (bool, str) tuple).
32	3. If it produces the same CSV schema (Frame,Visibility,X,Y), reuse or re-export
33	   TrackNetRunner.parse_trajectory_csv and .trajectory_to_action_windows (they are
34	   deliberately static and model-agnostic). If the new detector needs different
35	   windowing, it can implement its own or we generalize later.
36	4. Lazy-import heavy native deps inside the methods (so importing the segmenter
37	   registry never pulls torch/tf on a machine that doesn't have them).
38	5. Update the two hybrid segmenters (or introduce a detector= choice) to accept
39	   DetectorRunner instances (they already do the right thing via duck-typing + the
40	   type hints we add here).
41	6. Add unit tests that exercise the contract (isinstance, healthcheck shape, run
42	   returns str|None) using mocks for the external bits — see test_tracknet_runner.py.
43	7. No change to eval/calibration code paths (they consume the CSV + the static
44	   windowing helpers directly).
45	
46	Optional Linux-native path (the main de-risk goal):
47	A LinuxNativeRunner (or ONNXRunner) would:
48	- Skip all wsl / _stage / conda activate.
49	- Load weights locally (torch or onnx) or call a native binary.
50	- Write the exact same CSV format so trajectory_to_action_windows works verbatim.
51	- healthcheck can just check "weights file exists and torch importable + CUDA?".
52	This removes the WSL requirement for Linux users / future SaaS workers while
53	keeping the exact same candidate quality characteristics (or better, once native
54	weights are available).
55	
56	Error surfacing into reports:
57	The healthcheck message is already logged by the hybrids on failure. The reporting
58	harvest sees the outcome as "0 candidates" + detector name. When we wire richer
59	segmenter Results (or pass detector_health into emit_indexer_report context), the
60	(ok, msg) can be recorded explicitly under segmentation.details or a new detector
61	stage. The ABC makes that future change local to one place.
62	
63	See also:
64	- backend/pipeline/segmenters/{tracknet_hybrid,wasb_hybrid}.py (the two callers)
65	- tests/test_tracknet_runner.py (contract + pure logic tests; hybrids mock the runner)
66	- docs/T...

description: Commit hybrid-twin collapse and push
command: git add backend/pipeline/segmenters/trajectory_hybrid.py backend/pipeline/segmenters/tracknet_hybrid.py backend/pipeline/segmenters/wasb_hybrid.py backend/pipeline/detectors/base.py backend/pipeline/detectors/tracknet_runner.py backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_runner.py tests/test_tracknet_hybrid.py tests/test_trajectory_hybrid_equivalence.py mypy.ini docs/CODE_MAP.md && git commit -m @'
refactor: collapse the hybrid-segmenter twins into a shared trajectory base

tracknet_hybrid + wasb_hybrid were ~95% copy-paste; CODE_MAP carried the
standing footgun "any P3/P4 fix must be applied to BOTH". Now:

- NEW backend/pipeline/segmenters/trajectory_hybrid.py: the whole engine
  (healthcheck gate -> P2 trajectory->windows+persist -> P3 provider
  handoff -> P4 DB/link). Subclasses supply only family/display_label/
  name/description and _build_runner(idx_cfg) -> (runner, params extras).
- tracknet_hybrid/wasb_hybrid become ~40-line thin subclasses; behavior
  preserved (all 7 existing pipeline tests pass with only patch-target
  updates to the new module).
- shot_count drift resolved into one _shot_count_from: wasb relied on
  int(None) raising into the fallback (same result by accident);
  the explicit is-None branch is now canonical for both.
- _wsl_bash deduped: WslCommandMixin in detectors/base.py (beside, not
  on, DetectorRunner - future native runners must not inherit WSL
  plumbing); both runners switch, local copies deleted.
- DetectorRunner ABC now declares parse_trajectory_csv /
  trajectory_to_action_windows - the de facto contract the hybrids
  always called but the ABC never named (mypy caught it post-collapse).
- mypy quarantine RATCHETED: wasb_hybrid + tracknet_hybrid entries
  removed (thin subclasses of the type-clean base); one explicit
  override-ignore remains for the documented Result-contract gap.
- NEW tests/test_trajectory_hybrid_equivalence.py: parametrized over
  both twins - detector identity into DB rows, shot_count behavior
  (4 cases), provider offset math, base-not-registered. Future
  divergence must be deliberate, in the base, for both at once.
- CODE_MAP 2.1/2.2 updated to match.

Suite: 351 -> 364 green (13 new). ruff + mypy clean.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

### 511. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.416Z)

[external_agent_tool_result]
True
usage: rally_seq_proto.py [-h] --manifest MANIFEST

PROTOTYPE: a learned per-FRAME rally segmenter (vs the unsupervised windowing
heuristic). Motivation (2026-06-08 finding): WASB tracks the shuttle well on
Kushagra (97% of GT rallies contain >=2 net-crossings) yet NO velocity-
windowing config segments it (per-video F1 ceiling 0.163), while it works on
Adarsh (0.73). The rally SIGNAL is in the trajectory; the hand-tuned heuristic
just can't extract it across footage. This prototype tests the hypothesis that
a LEARNED model on per-frame trajectory features recovers the rally segments —
including on the footage the heuristic fails on. Approach (deliberately small,
dependency-light: numpy + scipy + the repo's pure-python LogisticRegression —
no torch/sklearn): 1. Per-frame features over a local window from the WASB
[/external_agent_tool_result]

### 562. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.420Z)

[external_agent_tool_call: PowerShell]
description: Run suite, commit and open PR for tilde fix
command: python run_all_tests.py 2>&1 | Select-Object -Last 3; git add backend/pipeline/detectors/base.py backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_runner.py backend/pipeline/detectors/tracknet_runner.py tests/test_wsl_tilde_quoting.py && git commit -m @'
fix(detectors): keep ~ expandable in WSL staging/inference commands

shlex.quote("~/clips/x.mp4") single-quotes the whole path, so bash never
expands ~ and cp fails with "cannot create regular file `~/clips/...`".
This broke LIVE WSL staging for the DEFAULT ~/clips stage dir in BOTH
runners (and the default ~/models weights path in the inference argv) -
present since the 2026-06-07 restructure, masked because trajectories
were generated via manual WSL scripts and tests mock _wsl_bash. Found by
the first real end-to-end wasb_hybrid smoke run (post AI-7 collapse).

Fix: wsl_tilde_quote() in detectors/base.py - quotes only the part after
a leading ~/ so bash expands the home prefix while spaces/specials in
the remainder stay protected. Applied to every ~-capable arg in both
runners (stage dest, video, frames dir, weights, output dir).

New tests pin the helper semantics AND the command shape: no quoted
tilde may ever reach _wsl_bash again (stage + full inference argv).

Suite: 372 green. This closes the BACKLOG "shlex-quote WSL config
values breaks ~ expansion" item.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
'@ && git push -u origin fix/wsl-tilde-quoting && gh pr create --title "fix(detectors): keep ~ expandable in WSL staging/inference commands" --body @'
**Found by the post-AI-7 live smoke run** (the eval-checkpoint protocol the owner asked for): first real end-to-end `wasb_hybrid` invocation failed at WSL staging with `cp: cannot create regular file `~/clips/...``.

## Root cause
`shlex.quote()` on a `~/...` path single-quotes the tilde, so bash never expands it. Both runners staged to the **default** `~/clips` and pass `~/models/...` weights into the inference argv - the live trajectory-hybrid path has been broken this way since the 2026-06-07 restructure. It went unseen because all existing trajectories were generated via manual WSL scripts, and the unit tests mock `_wsl_bash`. (This is the BACKLOG item "shlex-quote WSL config values breaks ~ expansion" - now closed.)

**Not an AI-7 regression** - verified `_stage_video` is byte-identical to pre-sweep master; the refactor just enabled the first honest live run.

## Fix
`wsl_tilde_quote()` in `detectors/base.py`: quote only the remainder after a leading `~/`, keeping home-expansion while still protecting spaces/specials. Applied to every `~`-capable argument in both runners.

## Verification
- New `tests/test_wsl_tilde_quoting.py`: helper semantics (5 cases) + command-shape pins - no quoted tilde can reach `_wsl_bash` from staging or the full inference argv.
- Suite **372 green**; ruff + mypy clean.
- Live re-run of the wasb_hybrid smoke follows on this branch before merge.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
'@
[/external_agent_tool_call]

description: Query smoke-run results from the DB
command: $code = @'
import sqlite3, json
c = sqlite3.connect("output/sports_indexer.db"); c.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
vids = [dict(r) for r in c.execute("SELECT id, status FROM videos WHERE filepath LIKE '%smoke%'")]
print("videos:", vids)
if vids:
    vid = vids[0]["id"]
    segs = [dict(r) for r in c.execute("SELECT start_time, end_time, metadata FROM play_segments WHERE video_id=?", (vid,))]
    print("play_segments:", len(segs))
    for s in segs[:8]:
        print("  %.1f-%.1f  %s" % (s["start_time"], s["end_time"], json.loads(s["metadata"]).get("detector")))
    n = c.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM candidate_segments WHERE video_id=?", (vid,)).fetchone()[0]
    print("candidates:", n)
'@
$code | python -
[/external_agent_tool_call]

### 638. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.425Z)

Here's the complete status — **everything is done and merged except one PR that's minutes from closing**:

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| AI-1 hygiene → AI-6 config warning | ✅ Merged ([#90](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/90)–[#96](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/96)), all on green CI |
| Bonus: SQLite connection-leak fix | ✅ Merged ([#94](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/94)) — found while wiring coverage; 340 ResourceWarnings → 4 |
| AI-7 twin collapse | ✅ On master, 13 equivalence tests, mypy quarantine shrunk by two modules — with one process slip I owe you below |
| Eval checkpoint (offline) | ✅ LOVO reproduces **exactly 0.626** (Mahadevpura Gen-0 0.744 to the third decimal) — the sweep is numerically inert |
| Eval checkpoint (live) | 🔶 Last step in flight — details below |
| Node-24 actions chip | ✅ Your session merged it as [#98](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/98) |

**Two things you should know:**

1. **Process slip on AI-7:** after merging #96 my script left me on master, and the AI-7 commit was accidentally committed and pushed *directly to master* — skipping the PR gate. Everything in it was locally verified beforehand and master CI came back green on all four lanes, but it has no PR record. I chose not to force-rewind master (concurrent sessions were live — and indeed one later flipped this very checkout's branch), so it stands as a confessed convention violation rather than a hidden one.

2. **The live smoke earned its keep — it found two real, pre-existing bugs** that had silently broken the *live* `wasb_hybrid` path (masked for weeks because trajectories were always generated via manual WSL scripts and tests mock the WSL boundary): `shlex.quote` was strangling `~` expansion in staging (the deferred BACKLOG item, now closed), and the relative `output/wasb` dir resolved against the WSL cwd, so an 8-minute GPU run wrote its CSV inside WSL where Windows never looked. Both fixed in [#97](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/97) (CI green, suite 373, regression tests pin the command shapes). The verification run for the final Gemini phase got killed ~7 hours ago when the session went idle mid-flight; I've relaunched it just now with the warm GPU cache. P1/P2 are already proven live — healthcheck through the new base, trajectory at the correct path, and 9 candidate rows in the DB with byte-correct detector identity (just verified above).

As soon as this run confirms the Gemini handoff (≈5–10 min), I merge #97 and refresh the memory checkpoint — I'll report back automatically when it lands.

Final state: master at `31069cb`, **zero open PRs**, your two uncommitted PR-2 doc edits preserved untouched throughout, and the memory checkpoint + session handoff are refreshed.

Closing delta since my last report:

- **The Gemini phase verified live despite a 503 capacity wave.** Of 9 candidate windows, one handoff punched through the wave (the 6-attempt exponential backoff from #83 doing its job) and produced a complete P4 write: a play segment tagged `wasb-hybrid-gemini-flash-latest` with the candidate→segment link recorded. That closes the loop on every phase of the refactored trajectory base running against real WSL, real GPU, and the real provider. The 1/9 confirmation rate is provider weather — the same 503 storms documented in the Gemini battery — and the pipeline degraded exactly as designed.
- **#97 merged on green** (all four CI lanes), carrying both live-path fixes plus regression tests that pin the WSL command shapes so neither bug class can return silently.

The scoreboard for the sweep: **9 PRs merged** (#90–#97 mine, #98 your chip session), **suite 351 → 373 green**, CI grew from 2 to 4 lanes (ruff, mypy with a documented quarantine ratchet, coverage floored at 70%), one never-compiled file purged, three real runtime bugs fixed (SQLite connection leak, WSL tilde quoting, WSL relative-path output), and the quality evals re-verified to the third decimal (LOVO 0.626, Mahadevpura Gen-0 0.744) plus the first-ever verified live `wasb_hybrid` run.

On the original question that started all this: the repo now closes the gaps that held it at 8 — the static-analysis hole is gone, coverage can only ratchet up, the twin-drift footgun is structurally retired, and the live path is actually exercised. By my own rubric that's a **9**, with the honest caveat that the deliberately deferred items (packaging, import-sort/pyupgrade rule groups, the `extra='forbid'` flip, yolo_hybrid fold-in) are what stand between this and higher — all recorded in the handoff for whenever you want them.

### 668. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.427Z)

No regeneration needed — it was genuinely less severe than that, for two independent reasons, both now verified rather than assumed:

**1. The bug class was fail-closed, not fail-corrupt.** Both bugs made `run_predict` return `None`, which makes the segmenter abort with zero output ("WASB produced no trajectory; aborting"). The tilde bug failed before any inference ran; the relative-path bug let inference complete but left the CSV where Windows couldn't see it — so the caller still got nothing. There is no code path where either bug delivers a *wrong* trajectory to a consumer: a trajectory either arrived intact or never arrived at all. (The orphaned CSV from run #3 that I found inside WSL was itself complete and well-formed — just invisible.)

**2. The training corpus never came through that path anyway.** I just checked the manifest: all six trajectories point to `Annotation Setup/Collect/Trajectories/*_traj.csv` — the outputs of the manual WSL-side scripts (`run_wasb_*.sh`) that earlier sessions ran *inside* WSL, invoking `wasb_infer.py` directly. Those scripts bypass the Windows-side staging code entirely, which is precisely why these bugs stayed invisible until the first honest end-to-end run. The inference core that produced your corpus is the same `wasb_infer.py` the fixed live path stages — the fix changed where files land, not a single number.

Belt and braces on top of that: the post-sweep LOVO reproduction came back at exactly **0.626** with Mahadevpura at 0.744, computed from those very CSVs, and the 2026-06-10 audit had already independently spoof-checked their row counts and visibility stats against the documented values.

Worth distinguishing: the one historical bug that *was* in the training-data-poisoning class — detector artifacts keyed by file basename, where two `match.mp4`s could reuse each other's frames — was found by the audit and fixed back in #64, before the Gen-0 corpus was assembled, and the smoke run re-confirmed the video-id keying live (the staged video and CSV both carried the MD5-namespaced key).

The practical upside going forward: now that the live path actually works end-to-end, future corpus growth (the camera-pilot videos, new multi-court matches) can run through the pipeline proper instead of hand-maintained WSL scripts — same trajectories, one less manual step, and the candidates land in the fine-tuning table automatically.

### 693. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.429Z)

[external_agent_tool_call: PowerShell]
description: Open PR-2 and watch its CI
command: gh pr create --title "feat(api): chunked upload + highlight download (B2+B3, PR-2)" --body @'
Implements UI_PROPOSAL B2+B3 (UI_ALPHA_EXECUTION_PLAN PR-2): chunked upload (init -> sequential parts <=95MB streamed with per-part/total caps -> complete with MD5 identity, never-overwrite) landing under `security.upload_dir`; streaming `GET /api/highlights/{video_id}/{filename}` with safe-name + root containment. Static UI: fixes the pre-existing `rally_ids` -> `segment_ids` compile bug and adds a working browser download link. Upload state is in-process (single-box alpha; restart aborts partials); per-user partitioning lands with PR-3. 15 new tests.

## Design decisions (review points — from the Cowork handoff)
1. **Sequential parts only** (409 otherwise) — simplest correct protocol for the alpha; parallel/resumable = beta (GCS signed URLs).
2. **Caps abort the whole upload** (413 + state removed) — no half-poisoned uploads lingering.
3. **`complete` returns `{path, video_id}`; client then calls /api/process** — upload and processing stay decoupled.
4. **Never-overwrite rename** (`name-<id8>.mp4`) — two testers with `match.mp4` can`t clobber each other pre-PR-3.
5. **In-process upload registry** — deliberate (single box; mirrors #16`s executor reasoning).
6. ⚠ **Hosted-mode note:** when `security.allowed_video_root` gets set (PR-3/deploy) it must contain `upload_dir`, or /api/process rejects uploaded paths.

## Execution notes (Claude Code)
- Cowork sandbox was down, so the suite ran here first per the handoff Step 0: **388 passed** (15 new upload/download tests), **ruff + mypy + coverage lanes all clean**.
- One trivial fix applied per the handoff`s "fix trivial breaks yourself" rule: 3 ruff **B904**s in the new endpoints (`raise HTTPException ... from e` chaining — same convention AI-3 standardized repo-wide). No design-level changes.
- Carries the two doc edits preserved from the #89 split (DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN #16-merged banner; UI_ALPHA_EXECUTION_PLAN working-agreement rewrite), per plan.

**Per the working agreement: awaiting Avi`s review — not auto-merging this one.**

10	## 1. The one-line model (v2)
11	
12	Don't sell videos or subscriptions — **sell shelf space, and let model quality ride the shelf**:
13	free = **2 GB + the owned model**; paid = **more shelf + the best model**; the visible meter is
14	always storage. The social layer makes the shelf worth filling; the owned model makes the free
15	tier cost ~nothing; the end-state moves compute onto the customer's own hardware entirely.
16	
17	## 1a. The three coupled axes (one user-visible meter)
18	
19	| Axis | Free | Paid | End-state (serious users) |
20	|---|---|---|---|
21	| **Storage** (the meter) | 2 GB library | 10 GB+ / pooled | Mostly local disk; cloud = shareable library only |
22	| **Model** (rides the tier) | Owned model only — **never Gemini** | Best path (two-stage refine / frontier) | Best owned model, on-device |
23	| **Compute** (the cost) | Our GPU, capped runs | Our GPU, priority | **Their GPU** (USB-drive workflow) |
24	
25	## 2. The load-bearing decision: two tiers of object
26	
27	| Tier | What | Size reality | Stored? | Quota? |
28	|---|---|---|---|---|
29	| **Source** (raw match upload) | Processing INPUT | 1–4 GB each | Temporarily: kept through processing + correction window, then **auto-deleted** (e.g. 7 days or on "corrections confirmed") | Not by default; optional "keep my raw match" toggle = counts |
30	| **Library** (compiled reels, kept rally clips, thumbnails) | The PRODUCT | reel ≈ 50–80 MB (3–5 min 720p) | Durable | **Yes — this is what 2 GB means** |
31	
32	Why this split: 2 GB cannot hold even one raw match, but holds **~25–40 reels** — the quota is
33	only meaningful against outputs. It also kills the "free CDN" abuse vector for a storage-priced
34	product: **the library only ever contains pipeline outputs** — there is no path to park arbitrary
35	files in it. (Sources additionally pass the existing relevance validator.)
36	
37	So: *"Do we store the uploaded video?"* → **Yes, transiently — it's a processing input with a
38	retention deadline, not a possession.** The reel is the possession.
39	
40	## 3. Eviction policies = a pluggable registry (the repo's own pattern)
41	
42	`EvictionPolicy` ABC + registry, exactly like `segmenter_registry`:
43	`fifo` (oldest first) · `largest_first` · `smallest_first` · `least_visited` (view-counter) ·
44	`hard_stop` (no deletion — block new saves until user frees space or upgrades).
45	
46	- **Proposed default: `hard_stop`** — never silently delete someone's match memories; FIFO etc.
47	  are opt-in conveniences. (Co-design question #2.)
48	- **Grace flow:** over quota → banner + email with the exact list the chosen policy would evict →
49	  N-day grace (propose 7) → evict, logged in an `eviction_log`. Offer download-before-evict.
50	- `least_visited` needs a decision: owner views, public views, or both?
51	
52	## 4. CUJ after upload (end-to-end)
53	
54	```
55	record → upload source → async job (PR-1) → review/correct (corpus engine, PR-4)
56	   → compile reel(s) → reel lands in LIBRARY (quota meter ticks)
57	   → share: profile page / unlisted link / private
58	   → source hits retention deadline → auto-delete
59	   → library overflow → eviction policy (with grace)
60	```
61	
62	A Gmail-style **quota meter** is permanent UI. Profile = `khelsutra.guru/@handle`: reel gallery,
63	view counts (feeds `least_visited`), sport tags....
[/external_agent_tool_result]

`docs/STORAGE_SHARING_MODEL.md` (2026-06-12, owner brief) — the storage-anchored monetization mental model:
